# Common Time (chirpers10): the sound bank

The first job on this record is the sound (album.json "brief"): a new family of guitars, amps, basses and kits, built on what is below with the engine as it is now. Add every new patch, rack, chain and recipe to the table at the bottom as you make it: name, what it is, what it was built from, where it lives. New names only; never edit a patch below (six released albums render through the library, and `tools/libguard.py` refuses a change that moves them).

## What exists: the djent family (inventory by chirp-eco, 12 Nov)

Made for the Meshuggah reproductions and *Ferrous Bloom* between 13 and 29 Oct; the engine has had 389 commits since the last of them changed.

Built into the engine (no file in `patches/`; `chirp patch show NAME` prints them, and the library patches above extend them):

- `haake`: Haake-style metal kit, dynamic: every hit varies (pitch, tone, level) and velocity changes timbre (beater click, snare crack/wires
- `m8`: Meshuggah-style 8-string (F standard: F1 Bb1 Eb2 Ab2 Db3 Gb3 Bb3 Eb4): bridge-humbucker DI (pickup position, resonance) into a 3-s
- `m8-lead`: Fluid, saturated legato lead (Thordendal-ish) with delayed vibrato, ping-pong delay and a big room.
- `m8-lead-guitar`: Guitar take on the m8 lead (opt-in; m8-lead is the oscillator lead): the m8 string through a singing lead amp — legato notes are h
- `m8-lead-synth`: Synth take on a fluid, saturated legato lead (oscillators, the pre-2026 m8-lead) with delayed vibrato, ping-pong delay and a big r

Library parts: `songs/lib-meshuggah-brk.yaml`, `songs/lib-meshuggah-hook.yaml`, `songs/lib-meshuggah.yaml` (sections with both guitars, bass, sub and the kit; `tr=` transposes).

Racks they lean on: `rack:drum-bus-punch`, `rack:parallel-ny`, `rack:room-tight`. The first two carry their own dry signal: fine as an insert, but on a parallel send every hit is added twice (bd-e0185e). Build send-safe ones under new names if you want parallel drums.

Reference records for the genre envelope (`ref/ID.wav`, the reproductions' sources): broken-cog, dancers-to-a-discordant-system (9:36), in-death-is-death (13:22), they-move-below (9:35), bleed, the-faultless, ligature-marks, pravus. 35 Meshuggah reproductions are paused in the registry with their song files (`songs/ID.yaml`); they are measurements of those records, not material.

## The new family

| sound | patch / recipe | built from | what is new in it | lives in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| rhythm guitars, a left/right pair | `ct-chug-l`, `ct-chug-r` | `m8-obzen-l` / `-r` (string, compressor, cab blend, EQ, gate, plate kept) | the amp is the engine's white-box one: triode preamp stages into a push-pull power amp inside a feedback loop (18 / 20 dB) driving a speaker's impedance; the low string settles from 0.8 semitones sharp over 110 ms; more scrape, twice the sympathetic ring, a palm mute that lasts twice as long. Against two records' guitar stems (tools/riffpunch.py, old pair -> this): Bleed 41.4 -> 67.0, The Faultless 37.8 -> 69.1 (separator floor 67-86). What each part gave on its own is in the patch's `about` and in ~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c10 (cands.py: 30 candidates) | `patches/ct-chug-l.yaml`, `patches/ct-chug-r.yaml`; t1 tracks gl, gr, gl2, gr2 |
| bass | `ct-bass` | `bass-fat` (the m8-bass string and sine sub, crunch amp, 4x10) | re-voiced against two records' bass stems, which live at 80-1300 Hz with little above 2.5 kHz: sub 6 dB lower, more string through a hotter amp (gain 0.7, mix 0.6), +6 / +8 dB at 110 / 220 Hz, +5 dB at 900 Hz, -10 dB above 1.5 kHz, 4 kHz low-pass. 1/3-octave envelope error against the stems (bbench.py, bass-fat -> this): Bleed 9.4 -> 4.7 dB, The Faultless 8.4 -> 3.8 dB. The white-box amp parts all measured worse on a bass; not used | `patches/ct-bass.yaml`; t1 track bass |
| kit (dry: no bus effects) | `ct-kit` | `metal-kit-dry`; `metal-kit-phys`'s snare; `modal-tom`, `modal-crash`, `modal-china`, `modal-ride` | SNARE: the physical `drum` layer (stick, two coupled heads, shell, wires) tightened to a 0.18 s head and a 140 ms envelope, +5 dB body at 200 Hz. tools/drumpunch.py against two records' drum stems (obzen-kit -> this snare, old cymbals): Bleed 44.1 -> 58.7, The Faultless 47.1 -> 56.8; the snare alone 26 -> 67 and 23 -> 40. TOMS: struck membranes on F3 C3 Ab2 F2 C2. CRASH, CHINA, RIDE: modal plates, high-passed at 1.2-1.5 kHz, at the old cymbals' level. Not settled: the bench reads the kit a little worse with the modal cymbals than with the old ones (56.4 and 55.5 against 58.7 and 56.8; the kick's reading, not the snare's); kept for an ear to judge. Kick: metal-kit's (a physical kick and an EQ'd one measured no better) | `patches/ct-kit.yaml`; t1 tracks kick, snare, cym, toms |
| drums bus (a recipe, in the song) | room 0.7 s at 10%, `glue` 2.5:1 at -24 dB, then `clip` with its ceiling at -13 dBFS | - | the kit's four tracks go to one bus (`out: drums`) and the bus is soft-clipped about 5 dB, as metal records do to kick and snare peaks: the whole mix then reaches -10 LUFS on a plain gain, with no limiter work, so what the groove plays (the raw mix) has the release's dynamics | t1 `buses.drums` |
| swell and drop (in the song) | `ct-swell`, `ct-drop` (song-local patches) | noise layer; sine | a breath in before a landing (band-passed noise that swells while the note is held, its band rising, cut on the bar line) and the floor dropping on it (a sine falling an octave in 0.35 s). Before 1:06, 3:29, 6:40, 6:59 and the landing | t1 `patches`, tracks rise, drop |
| hats and ride faders (a recipe) | the kit's hats and ride on their own tracks (`hat` +14 dB, `ride` +6 dB) | - | at the cymbals' fader a closed hat read 27-29 dB under the mix and the ride 23 under (tools/presence.py): parts nobody could hear. Now 16 and 12-15 under. The scorer's side of the same lesson: with the hats and ride audible the share of sections that match an earlier one ROSE (0.70 to 0.75), because a section whose cymbals nobody could hear had counted as different | t1 tracks hat, ride |
| wall rig, a pair | `ct-wall-l`, `ct-wall-r` | the m8 string; the white-box amp parts (modern voicing, `stack: recto`, triode, `nfb: 8`); irs kitten-slayer + kaiju-tamer (left), wumbo + big-bubba (right) | for open chords and slow riffs left to ring, where ct-chug is for tight muted riffs: a stiffer, beating string (stiff 0.45, planes 0.4), a looser power amp (8 dB of feedback against 18-20), more sag and bloom, thicker cabs with more room, a slow gate, a longer plate. A TONE CHOICE: there is no bench for ringing chords. Measured only against ct-chug-l on the same chords, level matched: 2-5 dB more at 90-355 Hz, 1-2 dB less at 355 Hz-5.6 kHz. In the song: eleven's open chords (1:44, 7:20, 10:28, the approach), riff C itself (3:29) and the filled eleven riff (6:59), the chord over the bass's break (9:46), every big hit | `patches/ct-wall-l.yaml`, `ct-wall-r.yaml`; t1 tracks wl, wr |
| clean guitar | `ct-clean` | m8-clean's string; `pickup_hz`, `aperture`, `pickup_nl`, `planes`, `beat`; amp `twin` at `mix: 0.5` | the ticks. m8-clean was a bright pluck through chorus, delay and a big room; this is a guitar's direct signal (a neck single coil resonant at 6 kHz, a string that beats slowly), a soft compressor, a clean amp blended half with the direct signal, no cabinet, chorus; dry, so the song sends it to the delay and hall. A TONE CHOICE, not fitted. Against m8-clean on the same notes, level matched: 3 dB less at 180-355 Hz, 3.5 dB more at 1.4-2.8 kHz, 2 dB less at 5.6-9 kHz, 13 dB less above 9 kHz (a cab version lost 18 dB at 5.6-9 kHz and was dropped) | `patches/ct-clean.yaml`; t1 tracks cl5, cl7, cl11 |
| coincidence chimes (a recipe) | the library's `modal-glock` (a free bar on the modal layer) at -19 dB, pan -0.2, to the hall at -6 dB and the delay at -14 dB | - | the struck bar where two wheels coincide (motifs.md). Against the mix where it plays: 11 dB under in the opening, 12 under in the eye, level with the ticks (it was 7 and 10 under at -14 dB: louder than the ticks) | t1 track chime |
| the mix against eleven records (a recipe, in the song; v7) | three bus equalisers, one compressor, one fader | - | WHAT WAS MEASURED: where the whole mix's energy sits in nine bands, in three heavy stretches, beside the median of eleven Meshuggah records at 0:30-2:30 (the records agree with each other within about 1 dB in every band). v6 had 3.9 dB too much at 250-500 Hz and 1.9 too much at 500 Hz-1 kHz (boxy), 2.2 too little at 60-120 Hz (the bass's weight sat on its 44 Hz fundamental, which the records leave to the kick), 1.7 too little at 4-8 kHz. Each instrument group against its separated stem said where: all three had too much 250-500 Hz; the bass's 60-120 Hz was 5.5 dB low; the drums' 4-8 kHz 4.8 dB low. THE RECIPE: rhythm guitars to a bus `gtrs` with -5 dB at 350 Hz and -2.5 dB at 750 Hz; the bass to a bus `low` with -7 dB at 44 Hz, +7.5 dB at 90 Hz, -8 dB at 350 Hz, +4 dB at 1.5 kHz, then a compressor (8:1 above -26 dB), because the 90 Hz boost alone made the bass 6 dB louder on Db and Eb than on F (their fundamentals sit in it): with the compressor the spread across the five grounds is 1.7 dB (v6 2.2); the drums bus, after its clip, -6 dB at 400 Hz, +4 dB at 5.5 kHz, +1 dB above 9 kHz; the tune's fader 2.5 dB down where it plays over the riffs (it was the loudest part there). RESULT: every band within about 1 dB of the records' median (+0.7 -0.6 +0.7 -0.2 -0.6 +0.1 +0.3 +0.5 +1.1 dB from below 60 Hz to 8-16 kHz). The stems are a separator's (bs_roformer), so the per-instrument numbers lean on it; the whole-mix numbers do not. Tool: `tools/stembal.py` (promoted by chirp-eco, 12 Nov) | t1 buses gtrs, low, drums; the lead's lane |
| the bass's fader (a recipe; v8) | a gain on the `low` bus after its compressor, moved by a lane on the bus (`auto` works on a bus) | - | the bass's own compressor and the bus's level every note, so velocity and the track's gain do nothing to its level (a note at velocity 0.45 came out as loud as at 0.9). To ride the bass by section, ride it after the compressor. Used once: 2.5 dB down under the slow chords at 5:14 | t1 bus low |
| barked voice (a trial on 12 Nov; IN THE SONG since v11, after Harry's "try different options in different parts") | the library's `kidman` with words as phonemes (`ph` on each note), track gain +7 dB, a little hall | - | over the hardest stretch (8:53), thirteen short lines on the bar's own eighth-notes while the band turns the seven wheel. On by default since v11 (`C10T1_VOX=` turns it off), high-passed at 300 Hz (it put the stretch 1.6 dB over the records at 250-500 Hz; now 1.0). At +7 dB it sits 8.8 dB under the mix, the share the records give their vocal; that stretch measured 2 dB short of the records at 500 Hz-1 kHz without it. A speech recogniser on the voice alone got one line of thirteen nearly right ("all is then all" for "holds them all") and one repeated made-up sentence for the rest. On a minute of Bleed's vocal stem it gets no word of the lyric right either, but hears a string of different words: to it, ours is less like speech than the record's bark. | generator `vox()`, `VOX_LINES` |
| the kit's top octave: overheads (a recipe, in the song; v10) | a bus `oh`: a 0.7 s room, mix 1, low_cut 5 kHz, high_cut 12 kHz, `spread` 0.6; sends from the cymbals, hats and ride at +4.5 dB and from the snare and toms at -1.5 dB (not the kick); a song-local kit over ct-kit that re-places the china (+0.6 to +0.35), the ride's edge and bell (centre to +0.4 and +0.35) and the hats (centre to -0.45) | - | WHAT WAS MEASURED (tools/width.py and a left-against-right level, 6-16 kHz): v9's top octave was nearly mono (side 7.5-10.7 dB under mid in three heavy stretches) and 5 dB heavier on the right, where the china is. Six of the records read 3.2-5.0 dB under (median 4.8) and 0.7-2.7 dB to the right. Scaling the side signal up made it wide and 8 dB lopsided; a room INSIDE the drums bus at a high mix turned the dry kit down with it (mix is a cross-fade). A room BESIDE the bus, fed by sends, is what an overhead pair is. After: 4.1-4.3 dB under and 0.8-1.0 dB to the right; the drums bus's own top comes down by what the room adds (its 5.5 kHz boost +4 to +1 dB, its shelf +1 to -2 dB), so the mix is still within 1 dB of the records in every band | t1 bus oh; patch ct-kit-here (in the song) |
| three soft singers (v11) | the library's `voice-backing` at -27 dB, to the hall | - | in the eye, on the tune's second pass (6:03-6:30), in unison with the lead guitar, on "ah". 9.4 dB under the mix there, 3 dB under the lead (at 0 dB it was 21 dB over the lead: a voice patch's level is nothing like a guitar's; measure before the first render). `C10T1_CHOIR=` turns it off | t1 track choir |
| a whisper (v11) | `ct-whisper` (a song-local patch: `voice-speech` with breath 0.95 and effort 0.05, which is what `chirp tts --mode whisper` builds), words as phonemes, syllable lengths from `chirp tts --print` | - | in the opening, half a bar before each new part comes in: "wait" (0:26), "wait" (0:41), "not yet" (0:56). 5 dB under the mix for the first (about 6 for the second, not re-measured), 10 under for the third (the guitars are coming in). `C10T1_WHISPER=` turns it off | t1 track whisper |
| bite rig, a pair (v12): THE SEVEN WHEEL'S GUITAR | `ct-bite-l`, `ct-bite-r` | ct-chug's string and amp chain with the engine's `nolly` amp voicing, the ENGINE'S OWN cabinet (`cab` 2x12, no cabinet file) and every note a down-stroke (`play.pick: down`) | riffpunch against two records' guitar stems, ct-chug first: Bleed 67.0 -> 66.8, The Faultless 69.1 -> 64.4 (the measure's noise is about 3). Alone on riff B, level-matched, it is the lean, biting one: -3 to -5 dB at 125-500 Hz and +2 to +4 dB at 1.6 and 3.15 kHz against ct-chug (ct-wall is the thick one, +4 to +5 dB at 160-315 Hz); `~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c10/rigdiff.py`. Tried and not used: the gojira voicing on `cab` 4x12 (60.0 / 56.8), the string's physical palm mute (Bleed 67 -> 34 alone at full depth: 400 Hz-1 kHz 13 dB too scooped against the record's mutes; reported to chirp-eco), a bridge pickup position (56 / 61) | t1 tracks sl, sr: every seven-wheel riff (the seven break 2:13, riff B 2:26, the right side of the split 4:26, seven over eleven 7:20, the right side with all three 7:46, the hardest stretch 8:53, the swap's left side 10:00, the approach). +4.3 dB to stand as loud as ct-chug on the same riff |
| the swarm (v13): the engine's model of synchrony | `ct-swarm` (a song-local patch after the library's `kuramoto-choir`): sixteen saws to a note, each at its own pitch (60 cents' spread), pulled toward their common phase by a `coupling` that the track's lane sets (0 a crowd, 1 the critical point, 3 locked); a low-pass opens as they agree. A second lane is its fader | - | on F3 and C4. THE OPENING (0:00-0:57): in step at bar 1, drifting apart over the wait. THE APPROACH TO THE END (11:06 on): a crowd, half-agreeing under the band, pulled into step over the two bars of the stop (12:09) and locked from the landing to the last bar. Measured alone (`~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c10/swarmchk.py`): the share of the energy within 6 cents of F3 falls 0.99 -> 0.14 over the opening and rises 0.12 -> 0.9 over the approach; the level's wobble (the beating) 0.3 dB in step, 1.0 dB as a crowd. Its level: with the drone's fifth in the opening, 4 dB over the ticks in the approach. `C10T1_SWARM=` turns it off | t1 track swarm |
| a phase-distortion synth on riff C (v13) | the library's `cz-bass` (wM), unchanged, high-passed at 420 Hz, -12 dB | - | doubles riff C (3:29-4:26) note for note two octaves above the wall guitars: a resonant sweep on every strike. 3 dB under each guitar. It fills a hole: riff C read 3.2 and 2.5 dB under the eleven records at 500 Hz-1 kHz (the wall rig is thick below and scooped there; I had never measured this section against them), now 0.3 and 0.5 under. `C10T1_CZ=` turns it off | t1 track cz |
| crunch rig, a pair (v14): A DIFFERENT GUITAR, AND AN AMP THAT ANSWERS THE PICK | `ct-crunch-l`, `ct-crunch-r` | THE STRING: a single coil at the bridge (pickup 0.06, no second coil, its resonance at 5.2 kHz), a thin pick near the bridge. THE AMP: `crunch` voicing, JCM800 tone stack (`stack: marshall`), triode stages, 8 dB of feedback, GAIN 0.13, no screamer, no compressor; the engine's 4x12; a mid lift after. Not through the guitars' bus (its eq scoops the mids this rig is made of) | TOUCH (open Ab1, a soft stroke against a hard one): 5.8 dB at gain 0.05, 4.0 at 0.12, 1.9 at 0.2, 0.5 at 0.3, 0.0 at 0.5; ct-chug 0.1. So this is the one rig where the playing's dynamics come through the amp. The voicing was set at gain 0.5 against Tool's Pneuma (`~/.local/state/chirper-zero/c10/pbench.py`: the record's guitar stem, riffpunch's measures): 400 Hz-1 kHz against 1-4 kHz from -13 to -5 (the record -3.6 and -5.1). Alone on the break against ct-bite: 5-11 dB more at 250-500 Hz, 6-13 dB less under 160 Hz, crest 17.6 against 14.0 dB. chirp-eco's `showcase.py --diff` prompted it: ct-bite was ct-chug's guitar through another amp; through a saturated amp a changed string moved the spectrum by 2 dB at most (cands.py sweep 11) | t1 tracks kl, kr: the first break (2:13, the chugs soft and the hook notes hard) and the first guitars back in the approach (11:20-11:31). +2 dB |

Measured alone: the balance of 1.5-3 kHz against 400-800 Hz swings from -3.8 dB at the heel to +9.9 at the toe (without the wah 3.8 to 8.0, from the notes alone) and follows the pedal (correlation 0.59 against 0.27). Level as before (5.3 dB under the mix; 5.5). `C10T1_WAH=` turns it off | t1 track leadw |

Measured on the guitars alone: the level above 1 kHz swings 2.0 dB at 0.8 Hz in the slow part and 4.3 dB at 6.7 Hz in the fast part (the engine's stated horn speeds); the drum reaches 4.8 Hz. THREE MEASURES, each with a tool in `compose/chirpers10/src/`. WORDS (`barktest.py`): a speech recogniser (small.en) reads 26 of the lyric's 35 words from the voice alone; 16 before. It still hears "none of them" as "not again", "one hour" as "come on out here" and "keep count" as "keep going". The smaller model is no judge of a scream (5 and 18 of 35 for the same sound at two levels). TONE (`voxref.py`): against the voices on eleven records (their vocal stems while they sing) the bark was 4.7 dB heavy at 250-500 Hz, 3.7 dB short at 1-2 kHz and 4.1 dB over at 4-8 kHz; now within 1 dB in every band. LEVEL (`voxref.py`): while it sings the records' voice is 9.2 dB under the record and 7.1 dB under it at 1-4 kHz (middle halves 0.8 and 0.7 dB wide); ours was 6.8 and 6.9, now 8.3 and 7.0 - so it came DOWN 3 dB overall and stayed where it was in the band that carries words. THE CONTROL (`refread.py`): in the full mix the recogniser reads none of our bark, before or after - and on five of the records it reads 0, 9, 12, 14 and 24 of the 49-65 words it reads from their vocal stems. So a recogniser is a judge of a harsh voice alone, not in the mix. Its noise, roughness and tilt (`tools/voxprofile.py`) are unchanged: harmonic-to-noise -0.9 dB (-1.6), subharmonics -5.4 (-3.7), tilt -5.6 dB/octave (-5.9). Caveat: the records' voices are a separator's stems. `C10T1_BARK=` gives the bark as it was | t1 track vox |

| the stutter (v21) | the engine's `stutter` on the master: slice 1/16, 5 repeats, each re-read a semitone higher; a lane (`auto.stut`) arms it for one slot | - | the first stop-time's last stab (3:29) becomes five, climbing four semitones into riff C, where there had been three sixteenths of silence. THE MIRROR of the tape stop at the second stop-time (8:52): the form is a palindrome and these are its two hinges; at one the pitch falls and time drags, at the other time splinters and the pitch climbs. The slot is five sixteenths, the five wheel's own turn, so it falls on the wheel's stab by itself. Measured: five hits at the stab's own level (-20 dB), brightness rising 1715 -> 2157 Hz, riff C's first bar untouched; idle it is bit-transparent. A TRAP: the effect reads `chance` once a block of about 5 ms and decides a slot at its first frame, and a lane moves in a straight line between its points; so a lane that rose 5 ms before the slot was read near 0, and its fall was read near 1 at the NEXT slot (riff C's first note stuttered). The lane is now up half a beat early and down half a beat before the next slot. (chirp-eco read the cause in the engine; I had called it smoothing.) `C10T1_STUTTER=` turns it off | t1 master |

| the ghost (v22) | `ct-attractor` (song-local): the engine's `chaos` layer, a Roessler system looping at the note's pitch, x axis; its `amount` on a lane (`auto.lock`); -20 dB, +3 dB once the first guitars are in; to the hall | - | the approach's first thirteen bars (11:07-11:31), over the ticks: it plays the END of the tune, from its fourth note, and stops where the band comes in and the lead begins the tune again from the top. THE ROUTE BACK FROM CHAOS, measured on its notes (share of the tone at fractions of the note): bars 350-353 it repeats every FIVE cycles (13-33% of it at fifths of the note: on F that is Ab, Db, Ab, B around the note); 353-356 every THREE (10-37% at thirds: a fifth below, a fourth above); 357-359, as the seven wheel's guitars enter, every TWO (3-7% at halves: the octave below, faint); from 359.6, as the five's enter, one clean tone. The Roessler map, for anyone using the layer (held Ab4; share of the tone off the whole multiples): 0-0.3 clean; 0.34-0.58 period two (4-6%); 0.66-0.75 chaotic (5%); 0.8-0.85 period three (17-18%); 0.9-0.95 chaotic (4-9%); 1.0 period five (19%). `C10T1_ATT=` turns it off | t1 track att |

| THE OTHER HELD CHORDS (v24) | faders again: a second lane (`ring`, with `"curve": 0`) on the guitars' bus and the bass's bus. The piece's FIRST chord (everything strikes once and holds two bars) falls 13 dB in a straight line from its third beat to its end; the chord held under the bass break (9:46, bars 308-314) falls 12 dB over the break | - | BEFORE: both sat flat at full level and cut off (the first chord: -12 dB in the file for seven quarter-bars, then -17, then the ticks at -24). NOW the first chord reads -12 -12 -13 -15 -16 -18 -20 -22 and meets the ticks at -24 as it ends; under the bass break the chord is 2.4 dB under the bass in the first bars and 9 under in the last (was 1.4 under throughout), so the break ends on bass and drums. These were the last two: a scan of the song finds four guitar notes a bar or longer (bars 0, 308, 350, 401) and all four now die. A LESSON ABOUT LANES, measured on a sine: a lane is an envelope, and its FALLING segments take the envelope's `curve` (default 3: fast, then slow; rising ones are straight). 0 to -20 dB over eight beats reads -3.0 -8.8 -12.6 -15.3 -17.1 -18.3 -19.2 -19.8, half the fall in the first fifth; with `"curve": 0` on the lane it reads -1.2 -3.7 -6.2 ... -18.7, the line as written. The default on a chord gives a quick drop and a plateau, not a decay, so these two use the straight lane. v23's ending and every earlier fade keep the default, each described as measured; the ending as a straight line exists as a clip to compare (`C10T1_ENDLINE=1`). `C10T1_RINGS=` gives the two chords held flat | t1 buses gtrs, low |
| THE PICK (v25) | the five wheel's rig (tracks gl, gr, gl2, gr2) with `synth.layers.0.soft=expr.pm fx.2.tight=220` and an eq after it on each track (100 Hz +2.2 dB, 280 Hz -3 dB). `soft` is the pluck's excitation: 0 a burst of noise, 1 the triangle of a string let go. Bound to the palm mute, so only muted notes change | `ct-chug-l/r` (overrides at the song; no library patch touched) | WHAT IT DOES: with the noise pick a muted low F is flat to 1 dB from its start to the next note (a hiss the amp holds). With the triangle it pulses at the string's own rate, every 23 ms: 2 dB in the whole band, 6-7 dB at 100-250 Hz. A growl. AGAINST THE RECORD (tools/riffpunch.py, Bleed 0-30 s, 302 muted notes, this rig; record in brackets): rise 20.2 -> 15.5 ms (14.2), low end early against late 3.7 -> 1.4 dB (1.2), noise between harmonics 0.26 -> 0.40 (0.41), punch 4.1 -> 4.0 (3.6), 400 Hz-1 kHz against 1-4 kHz -11.2 -> -9.9 (-11.9). On every note it is WRONG (The Faultless's open notes: punch 5.8 against 4.0): muted notes only. ONE MEASURE AGAINST: guitarbench's low-mid contrast 18 dB against the record's 27 (v24's rig 27); not the pick noise, grain, ring or buzz. THE TONE IS HELD: a muted note's direct signal is fundamental-heavy (+8 dB at 80 Hz after the amp), so `tight` goes 80 -> 220 Hz and the track eq takes back what that leaves; the guitars alone are within 0.5 dB of v24 on average over the third-octaves (1.9 at the worst, 630 Hz), every section within 0.5 dB in five bands, and no band of any guard window moves more than 0.6 dB against the records. Seven's and eleven's rigs keep the noise pick: at 4:26 and 7:46 the two attacks are side by side, left and right. The bench cannot tell a muted string from an unmuted one once `soft` is on, so it is no judge of the mute's decay. `C10T1_PICK=` gives v24 | t1 gl, gr, gl2, gr2 |

| THE BASS GROWS TEETH (v27) | a bus `grind` fed by a SEND from the bass track (the same performance to the sample; a second bass track would be humanized apart and comb against the first): `eq hp 300` -> `gain` on a lane (`auto.teeth`: +30 dB at bar 308.5 -> +50 at 314) -> `drive shape=fold drive=0` (the engine's wavefolder) -> `eq hp 300, lp 4500` -> `gain` on a lane (`auto.lvl`: -120 dB everywhere but the break; -34 at bar 308.6 -> -26 at 312.5, held, cut at 315) | - | the bass break (9:46-10:00): as the held chord dies (v24) the bass turns from round to snarling and takes the room the guitars leave. MEASURED on the bass alone, by half bars, what the bus adds: -45 -43 -40 -39 -38 -36 -36 -34 -33 -33 dB beside a dry bass at -28; in the last two bars it is 8-10 dB ABOVE the bass's own 320 Hz-2.5 kHz and 23 dB above it at 2.5-5 kHz (those are fold products: the dry bass is low-passed at 4 kHz). In the mix: the bass's presence in the break's second half -4.8 -> -3.6 (now the loudest part; the kick -4.7 -> -5.4); the section +1.4 dB at 355 Hz-1.4 kHz and +1.1 at 1.4-5.6 kHz; every other section 0.0. `C10T1_GRIND` = hp : gain in at the start : at the end : lp : level; "" = v26 | TRAP, found here: the folder's own `drive` must not be swept. A fold's output is scaled by 1 / abs(sin(pi k / 2)), k = 1 + 19 drive + 80 drive^3, which has a pole at every even k: with `drive` on a lane the bus hit the master's ceiling in spikes while adding 0.5 dB. Leave `drive` at 0 (k = 1, scale 1) and sweep the level going IN (a `gain` ahead of it): the signal folds once past 0 dBFS, twice past +6 dB, and so on. No record to compare a bass break against: kept on the measurements above and nothing else

The piece is a palindrome, and on a loop the fall runs into the rise. MEASURED (polechk.py: the part with the pole over the part without, in twelfth-octave bands, frame by frame): the pad alone, a comb 13.5 dB deep climbing an octave in about 5 bars in the wait and falling at the same rate in the ring-out; in the whole mix the comb is 2.0 dB deep in the wait and 2.1 in the ring-out. The pad's level is held in the wait (-38.7 LUFS alone, -38.9 before); the ring-out's pad, struck softer than the wait's, gets 3 dB back so it stands beside its ticks as the wait's does. Its presence: wait -9.2 (the ticks -8.8). Every section but those two is 0.0 dB different. `C10T1_POLE` = bars per octave : q : mix : width : make-up rising : make-up falling; "" = v27 | TWO THINGS LEARNED. (1) At the effect's default `q` (2) on this pad NOTHING moved that could be measured: bands an octave apart with q 2 overlap into a nearly flat sum (comb 7.7 dB, no shift from frame to frame). q 8 is where a pole appears. (2) A pole is invisible to octave-band meters (each octave always holds exactly one band): measure it in twelfths of an octave, against the dry part. Tried and taken out: the same pole under the approach's first bars (11:06) was 0.7 dB deep in the mix, with the pad at -15.6: not there to be heard. An effect whose `mix` lane is at 0 is not bit-exact with no effect: the eye's pad differs from v27's at 93 dB under its own level

(1) The three clean guitars, everywhere: `ct-clean`, `ct-glass`, `ct-bell` each with `synth.layers.0.inharm=0.00008` (tracks cl5, cl7, cl11, e5, e7, e11). With `inharm` a rendered string's fitted B is within 5% of the value asked from F1 to E4 (bfit.py). THE CLEAN GUITARS alone (bars 23-29): the same level (-34.2 LUFS both), within 0.4 dB up to 2.5 kHz, 1.5-2.1 dB more above 3 kHz. THE FIVE WHEEL, on the Bleed bench, v25's rig -> this one (record): roughness at 1-4 kHz 0.40 -> 0.33 (0.41; the old noise pick 0.26); low-mid contrast at 200/400/800 Hz 22/18/15 -> 27/25/20 (32/27/24); scoop -9.9 -> -10.3 (-11.9); tight 1.4 -> 1.2 (1.2); punch 4.0 -> 3.9 (3.6); rise 15.5 -> 17.5 ms (14.2). So it gives back most of the one measure that read against v25's pick, at the cost of some of the roughness v25 gained: two points on one trade, and the song now has both, one each side of the eye. In the song (riff A's return, guitars alone, muted low F): tight -0.2 (v25's rig -0.2, noise pick 2.0), roughness 0.35 (0.41, 0.30); tone within 0.6 dB of the noise pick's in third-octaves. Guard windows after the eye against the records: landing riff 500 Hz-1 kHz -1.1 -> -0.2 and 120-250 Hz +1.8 -> +1.6; riff A's return 500 Hz-1 kHz +0.4 -> +1.0; the rest within 0.2. `C10T1_REAL` (the cleans' B) and `C10T1_REAL5` (B ; pick ; tight ; eq ; from bar); "" = v28 | LEARNED: with the string pick at full and the white noise on, `inharm` does not help (rise 25 ms, contrast 25/19/17); with no white noise and the full pick the roughness collapses (0.24): the 30% of noise burst left in the pick is what keeps it. White noise and low-mid contrast trade directly against each other; `inharm` buys roughness more cheaply (0.22 -> 0.34 for 7 dB of contrast; white noise 0.22 -> 0.26 for 3-4). One B for a patch, not one per string; nothing here is heard

| CORRECTION (14 Nov 01:25), no change to the song | - | - | MY SLIP in the v35 row and note: 'the engine's spring is three springs whose highs arrive after their lows, so a muted note leaves a short falling splash'. I said that from the source's comment and never measured it. chirp-eco did (tools/dispersion.py, an impulse through the effect): every band from 250 Hz to 5.7 kHz arrives together (11.5-11.8 ms) and every echo lines up, as a plain delay's does. Its twelve allpasses are first-order and differ by 0.004 ms between 500 Hz and 4 kHz; a spring's chirp needs about a hundred, stretched. So v35's 'spring' is three short loops' flutter, darker than the plate: a different reverb, which does what I measured (the gaps 3 dB up, the mute kept), but NOT a drip | The engine has no dripping spring yet: bd-7becc2 (mine now), opt-in, today's `spring` kept bit for bit
| THE LANDING RIFF'S OWN RIG (v37) | tracks `el`/`er`/`el2`/`er2` (`C10T1_LANDRIG` = `gojira:0.76;0.25,6,7;0.5,1.5`: amp model : mid ; mic , left cone , right cone ; trim dB left , right) | the real-string tight rig (`ct-chug-l/r` + the v29 string) with `fx.2.model=gojira fx.2.mid=0.76`, the engine cabinet `4x12-modern` mic 0.25 cones 6 / 7 | WHY: a wheel is known by its rig, and the landing's riff is no wheel (its cell is eight sixteenths, common time itself); until v36 it played on the five wheel's rig. Now it has one heard nowhere else: the engine's eighth amp model, the last one the piece had not used (showcase: amp models 8 of 8). THE FOUR GUITARS ALONE (bars 389-395, gspec --band, against the five wheel's rig at the same loudness, -18.9 LUFS, each side trimmed to what the five wheel's rig was on that side: -22.0 left, -21.8 right): 71-141 Hz -1.0 dB, 141-282 Hz -2.8, 282-560 Hz +1.2, 560 Hz-1.1 kHz +1.3, 1.1-2.2 kHz +1.0, 2.2-4.5 kHz +0.6, 4.5-9 kHz +0.8: mids forward, less low-mid. IN THE MIX (stembal --voice-tracks, the landing riff against the records' passages with no voice): 120-250 Hz +2.0 -> +1.0, 500 Hz-1 kHz -0.7 -> +0.2, 1-4 kHz up 0.4-0.5, the other bands within 0.1 of where they were; all nine bands 0.69 -> 0.54 dB off. No other section moves (sectab: 0.0 everywhere else). EACH PART AGAINST THE MIX (presence, bars 389-400): kick -7.3 loudest, er -7.5, el -7.7, snare -8.1, bass -8.2, cym -11.3, er2 -11.4, el2 -11.7: the balance v30 set (kick -7.3, rl -7.6, rr -7.8, snare -8.1, bass -8.2) | NOT HELD: (i) against the record's guitar heard alone (cabspec) the rig is FURTHER than the five wheel's: 1.62 -> 2.39 dB mean, 630-800 Hz 5.5 over (the records' guitars are scooped there; this rig is not, on purpose: it is a different guitar). (ii) the low end's stereo (lowwide, landing riff): 110-180 Hz side minus mid -3.6 -> -5.6 and 180-300 Hz -2.5 -> -4.0 (records' medians -4.1 and -2.4): the guitars carry less of the low-mids, as in riff C (-5.7 / -5.3). The amp model does that more than its mid knob (mid 0.8 read -6.0 / -4.7 and its mix row +0.7 at 120-250 Hz). LEARNED: the landing's 2 dB too much at 120-250 Hz was in the centre AND the sides alike (the stereo ratio was the records'); taking it from the guitars alone mends the total and narrows the ratio. If the landing feels less wide, the other half is the bass ride (LANDMIX's +1.5 dB). (iii) with ONE amp model on both sides the right guitar, whose patch sets its own bass and presence, came out 0.9 dB under the left: a rig built by override wants each side levelled alone. (iv) tools/levels.py's first figures are peak and true peak; LUFS is the fourth column (I matched peaks first and caught it on the presence table)
| ODD SYMPATHY (t2 v1), the skeleton's palette | tracks `gl` / `gr` (heavy), `cl` / `cr` (clean), bass, the kit, `home`, `air`, `swarm` | Common Time's: the tight rig on the engine cabinet (`ct-chug-l/r` by override, `cab 4x12-modern` cones 12 / 13), one guitar a side and no doubles; `ct-clean` with the real string; `ct-bass`; `ct-kit` with the kick's click; the swarm | NEW IN USE: (i) the engine's track `warp` on `gr` and `cr` (the right-hand clock): ten stretches, holds and slips; measured with lagchk.py (the two channels' envelopes cross-correlated): the right guitar leads by 0.03, 1.04, 2.03, 3.02, 4.00 and 7.99 sixteenths at the six stations. (ii) the spring's `drip` (engine feature 5) on the clean guitars in the drift: `reverb kind=spring drip=1 decay=1.8s mix=0.22`, its first use in a song | LEARNED: a riff written with each note lasting to the next has NO holes through a saturated amp (the level sat within 3 dB all bar); the holes need notes one or two sixteenths long, and even then the amp's tail fills half of a one-sixteenth rest (dips of 12-25 dB)
| ODD SYMPATHY (t2 v2), the drift | track `tick` (`ct-kit`'s ride bell) with the engine's track `risset` (`C10T2_DRIFT` = `0,-2,-4,-5;16:2;0.5`: the roots ; cycle beats : copies ; pedal velocity) | the pendulum, bars 80-96 | THE RISSET PENDULUM, first use of `risset` in a song. Read from the track alone (onsets): in each four bars the ticks' gap closes 0.47, 0.44, 0.40, 0.39, 0.34, 0.26 of a bar while they fade (-2 to -13 dB), a second tick-tock fades in at 0.70 of a bar and closes 0.62, 0.57, 0.51, and the tick lands on the bar line at 84.00, 88.00, 92.00 and 96.00, where the plain pendulum takes over | LEARNED: (i) with TWO copies a cycle starts with the written-speed copy alone, so it can hand to and from a plain pattern without a seam; with three or four, copies at other speeds are already sounding at the boundary. (ii) that copy starts part-way through the pattern (0.5 x cycle / ln 2 beats in, modulo the pattern's length), so the track's `at` wants to be early by cycle x log2(1 + (L - p0) x ln 2 / cycle) beats (0.454 here) to put its first tick on a bar line; then every cycle's first tick is on one. (iii) `length` cuts the last cycle where the plain pattern resumes

LEARNED: (i) the amp's `presence` does nothing under its feedback loop (0.1 dB), as `resonance` did nothing in Common Time: the top is the cabinet's microphone (`mic` 0.3 -> 0.4: 2.2-4.5 kHz -0.7 dB, 4.5-9 kHz -1.3). (ii) matching two rigs by LUFS hands back most of a 2-4 kHz cut, because that band IS the K-weighted loudness of a guitar: to lower a mix's 2-4 kHz, lower the guitars. (iii) a bus reverb's `mix` turns the dry down too (0.06 cost 0.5 dB). STILL OPEN: 2-4 kHz is 1.3-2.0 dB over in three windows; the hold an eighth apart is dark on top (its hat: 8-16 kHz -2.8)

LEARNED: (i) a send from a track on a CLIPPED bus to an unclipped one is a path round the clip: the track's level then lives in the send. Check a part alone with the send out before trusting its fader. (ii) [CORRECTED 14 Nov 11:50: I had written that stembal reads loud frames; I had not tested it and it is wrong.] stembal reads the MONO SUM of the mix (chirp-eco, from its code), and a wide room counts short in mono where a dry centred drum counts whole. Tested on riff A, the candidate without the track's shelf minus v40, at 8-16 kHz: both channels' power +0.27 dB, the mono sum +0.54 (stembal had read +0.6). | EXTREME AND MEAN (t7 v13), four kettledrums | NEW ON THIS RECORD: the modal layer's `kettle` mode set (Rossing's kettledrum: the air in the kettle pulls the head's modes to 1 : 1.5 : 1.98 : 2.44, so the drum sings its note). Song-local `em-kettle`: one `modal` layer (`modes: kettle`, strike 0.72, decay 5 s, damp 0.2, sharp 10, vary 0.3) and a 16 ms brown-noise thud; hp 45 Hz, lp 2.4 kHz; `play.gate=1`, `play.poly=32`; `synth.pitch` follows `expr.pedal` in semitones, and a ringing head follows it. Track `kettle`, −12 dB, pan −0.1, hall send −12 dB. In the middle quiet section (2:38–3:05) it takes the kick's thirteen soft strokes (the kick rests there) and gives them the harp's tune on cells of 13 and 8: A♭2, B2, E♭3, G♭3. The last note is a roll of 20 strokes with the pedal falling G♭3 to F3 over the last half second, landing on F3 with the band. `C10T7_KETTLE=''` restores v12; its third field empty gives one stroke and no roll. | A stroke's principal is on the written pitch (103.8 and 155.4 Hz for A♭2 and E♭3); the modes at 1.5, 1.98 and 2.44 read −8, −12 and −23 dB under it, the 0.85 thump −19 dB and falling 10 dB in a tenth of a second; little above 500 Hz. Over the section the drums sit 3.6 dB under the two harps and 0.5 dB over the bowl (stroke peaks −19.8 to −16.8 dBFS on the bench; the harps' −15.4, the bowl's mallet −19.7); each stroke's first half second is 1.8 to 4.6 dB under everything else together. The section is 0.46 LU louder (4.4 dB under the band before it; it was 4.9). The roll murmurs at the level of the rest, then stands 5 to 6 dB over it for its last 0.4 s; solo pitch +100 cents until 0.56 s before the band, then +77, +51, +26, +6, 0. | THREE THINGS THE BENCH TAUGHT. (1) `bright` does nothing to a kettle: the striker's cut-off starts at 600 Hz and every kettle mode of these notes lies under it; `damp` is the tone control. (2) A ROLL'S STROKES MUST FALL IN STEP WITH THE HEAD. Every stroke is a new voice that starts its modes at the same phase, so strokes 75 ms apart at 185 Hz (13.875 cycles) cancelled: a hole 14 dB deep a third of a second in. Spaced a whole number of cycles apart they add; an EVEN number, because the second mode is a fifth up and after 13 cycles of the principal it is half a turn out (it vanished mid-roll). Strokes in step add up, so each needs a large trim (here −22 rising to −8 dB under a plain stroke). (3) `play.gate` defaults to 0.95, and an `expr_path` runs to the RELEASE: set `gate=1` on a patch whose path must end where the note is written to end. `play.poly` defaults to 16; a roll of 20 held strokes needs more. Not heard by me. |

(1) A CUT CAN GO INSIDE THE PIECE: `filters.2.*` overrides create a filter the piece did not have, so a track that carries two pieces (here the snare and the stick on the rim) can change one and leave the other bit for bit (the quiet walks null at −80 dB; with the rim split onto its own track for a track eq they came out as another random take, −28 dB). In the piece 8 dB at 650 Hz does what 5 dB does on the track. Unity is Q times 1.313, so the chain sits at -20 log10(Q / tanh 1) and the lane adds the rest. It grows 36.5 dB a second per dB over unity at 1.2 kHz and 43.8 at 3 kHz (the round trip is the return's time plus the band-pass's delay); the ceiling rises with the gain over unity (-17.9, -14.2, -11.3 dB rms at 0.1, 0.2, 0.4). THE LOOP BUS'S OWN FADER IS INSIDE THE LOOP: set the level on a second bus. A silent loop stays silent: it needs a seed (the last stroke), and the seed's size is when it arrives. Pitch = harmonic / time, to a cent. (1) THE KIT'S SNARE SOUNDS 1.76 SEMITONES OVER ITS WRITTEN NOTE (written A3, it sounds 243.5 Hz, B3 less 25 cents: the two heads' pair, raised by the air between them) and WANDERS 20 cents either way stroke to stroke; `pitch.mod.0.amount=0` and `pitch.mod.1.amount=0` stop the wander (to 3 cents) without moving the note. Its second mode is at 1.44 times the note, a tritone and 30 cents; `cavity` moves the note and leaves that mode where it is (written on one key, read dry: the note 164.4 Hz at 0.1, 170.3 at 0.2, 174.7 at 0.3, 179.4 at 0.45, the second mode 251 to 254 throughout; 0.162 makes the two a fifth), `reso` does not. `tune_sounding=true` on the drum leaves the note 25 cents flat and puts a second line at 0.69 of it, 0.3 to 2.2 dB under: not used. (2) MOST OF THE SNARE IS THE OVERHEAD ROOM: without its send `oh` the stroke's first 150 ms lose 10.7 dB and 15 to 17 dB from 1.25 to 3.15 kHz, so the humps the snare shows there beside the records (+5 to +10 dB at 1, 1.25, 2 and 2.5 kHz) are mostly the room's, and cures inside the drum reach little of them (a 5 dB cut at 1 kHz inside the drum: 3.7 dB dry, 2.1 through the chain). The bus's clip changes nothing in the shape, its glue about 1 dB. (3) SO GIVE THE SNARE A ROOM OF ITS OWN: a copy of the overhead room with the cuts after it; the cymbals keep theirs. READS here (snarespec, five stretches): 5.3-5.7 > 3.1-4.6; body against crack into the records' range in all five; wires against crack -5.1..-6.7 > -0.8..-1.7; the mix nearer the records in all six stretches on both yardsticks (voiceless 1.106 > 1.072, all eleven 0.928 > 0.850); the kit by part 500 Hz-1 kHz +5.5 > +3.5, 1-2 kHz +2.5 > -1.0. COSTS: the ring after the stroke 2 to 3.7 dB lower by the meter; a snare whose pitch can be named. Read a snare DRY (off the bus, no sends) to find its note: through the chain the room's own fixed lines (205, 225, 261, 290 Hz ...) are what a peak-picker finds. | a hi-hat that is a cymbal: the sticks' stroke and the foot's on ONE plate (The Other Hand v29, chirper-alpha) | kit pieces over `ct-kit`: `h` = `{type: modal, modes: cymbal, bright: 0.9, damp: 0.02, decay: 400ms, strike: 0.9, tilt: 1, vary: 0.5}` + a 4 ms noise tick by `env.att` (amount 40), `drive: 0.3`, amp `d: 70ms, curve: 2`, filters `hp 2500`, `peak 5500 q=0.8 +2dB`, `highshelf 9000 -9dB`, pitch C5, gain -16 dB under an 11 dB fader; `hp` = the same plate, `bright: 0.6, strike: 1`, no tick, amp `a: 0.5ms, d: 45ms`, `hp 1500`, `lp 9000`, gain -23 dB; both `choke: hat` | the modal layer's cymbal mode set (the album kit's crash and china), in place of the library's `haake-hat` (white noise and three squares through an 8 kHz high-pass) | NEW: THE STOCK HAT IS A TICK IN THE TOP OCTAVE AND NOTHING UNDER IT: 74% of its power over 8 kHz, 20 dB down in 20 ms, 11.2 dB from the records' drums' top (`cymspec.py`), and in the mix 3 to 7 dB UNDER everything else at 4–8 kHz. The plate: 54% at 4–8 kHz, 50 ms, 3.2 from the records (the foot 1.0), 1 to 5 dB over everything else at 4–8 kHz. A HAT'S LENGTH ON THIS LAYER: `damp` 0.1 to 0.5 with a 150 ms decay gave 10 to 21 ms; `damp` 0.02 with a 400 ms decay, amp `curve: 2` and a little `drive` gave 50 (40 without the drive): changed together, not one at a time. THE NOTE DOES NOT TUNE THE CYMBAL MODE SET (C5 and F5 within 0.3 dB by thirds of an octave): do not write a top and a bottom plate. SET ITS LEVEL BY THE MIX'S TOP OCTAVE (keep what the old hat gave 8–16 kHz); where the mix is already level at 4–8 kHz it goes 0.2 to 0.6 over. Still Owing and Slow Enough to Count are on the stock hat | `compose/chirpers10/src/chirpers10-t12.py` (`PLATES`); benches `~/.local/state/wM/common-time/t12-v29/` |

ITS RISE (3 to 12 ms; the records 13.5 to 23.5) moved with nothing: pick softness, palm filter, click, string attack. TRIM BY THE FINISHED FILE: the pair alone was 1.58 dB louder, and −1.6 dB left the guitar-free bars 0.09 dB high, −1.3 level. `sustain=0` and `-1` read alike.
